and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\circ(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\varphi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\circ(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\circ(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

Second,

\[
S_\bullet(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\bullet(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\psi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\bullet(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\bullet(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

These are the sums of the primitive and dominant schedules generated by the Zhang–Jiang concatenation dynamic program.

Let \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\) be any Zhang–Jiang dominant schedule attaining \(S_\bullet(n)\).

Their analysis gives the exact worst-case objective profile

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n\!\left(h_\bullet^{(n)}\right)
=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

The equality is important: the associated Huber instance attains the bound.

Define

\[
\boxed{
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\qquad
\text{for every }n.
\]

This exact sequence, rather than the decimal \(0.4239\), is the benchmark that must be matched.

---

## 3. Exact problem to resolve

Determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
}
\tag{P}
\]

Equivalently, determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
\frac{1-o(1)}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{LB}
\]

Since the reverse inequality

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}
\]

is already supplied by the Zhang–Jiang schedule, proving (LB) establishes asymptotic minimax optimality of their schedule horizon by horizon.

The quantifiers in (LB) are strict:

for every \(\varepsilon>0\), there must exist \(N_\varepsilon<\infty\) such that for every \(n\ge N_\varepsilon\) and every predetermined schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1-\varepsilon}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{Q}
\]

The threshold \(N_\varepsilon\) must be independent of the particular schedule \(h\).

The adversarial function and its dimension may depend on \(n\) and on \(h\).

---

## 4. What “matching the complexity and constant” means

A complete affirmative solution must prove (P), not merely the silver exponent.

In particular, since

\[
S_\bullet(n)=\Theta(n^\rho),
\]

(P) automatically implies the optimal polynomial convergence exponent

\[
\rho=\log_2(1+\sqrt2).
\]

It also matches the complete asymptotic constant profile, including any genuine oscillation of \(S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho\).

For reference, define

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
:=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{S_\bullet(n)}{n^\rho}
\]

and

\[
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2\bar\nu_\bullet}.
\]

Then

\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
\]

Therefore (P) implies

\[
\boxed{
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
}
\]

However, proving only this limsup identity is weaker than (P) and does **not** count as a complete affirmative solution.

Likewise, proving merely

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})
\]

does not count.

---

## 5. Important warning about the number \(0.4239\)

Do not formulate the theorem as

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\sim
0.4239\,n^{-\rho}.
\]

That is not the precise Zhang–Jiang theorem.

Their rigorous asymptotic statement is expressed through

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho
\]

and the coefficient

\[
(2\bar\nu_\bullet)^{-1}.
\]

The frequently quoted number \(0.4239\) comes from an additional numerical estimate for \(\bar\nu_\bullet\), and is not itself the exact rigorously identified asymptotic constant.

The paper also notes numerical evidence for still smaller constants.

Thus:

- \(0.4239\) may be used as a numerical sanity check;
- it may not be assumed as an exact constant;
- a proof that only matches \(0.4239\) numerically is not sufficient;
- the rigorous benchmark is \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}=(2S_\bullet(n)+1)^{-1}\).

---

## 6. Current lower-bound benchmark

A recent lower bound of Ye and Liu considers, after setting \(L=1\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup_{f,x_0}
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}
{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\qquad
r_n^\star
=
\inf_{h\ge0}\mathcal R_n(h),
\]

and proves that for some absolute \(C>0\),

\[
r_n^\star
\ge
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}
\]

for all sufficiently large \(n\).

Equivalently, in the normalization of this problem,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
2
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}.
\]

This establishes the correct polynomial exponent only up to a subpolynomial loss.

It does **not** establish (P).

Removing the exponent loss alone is also not sufficient: the leading asymptotic constant/profile must be matched.

---

## 7. Existing hard-function tool

The Ye–Liu lower bound may be used as a starting point.

For checkpoints

\[
1\le t_1<\cdots<t_k\le n,
\]

write

\[
b_i=h_{t_i},
\qquad
s_i
=
\sum_{j=t_{i-1}+1}^{t_i-1}h_j,
\]

with \(t_0=0\), and let \(s_{k+1}\) denote the sum of the steps after the last checkpoint.

For suitable parameters \(\epsilon,\gamma>0\), putting

\[
c=\sqrt{1+2\gamma+\epsilon^2},
\]

their construction yields constants \(a=a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) and \(B_0=B_0(\epsilon)\) such that, when every checkpoint obeys \(b_i\ge B_0\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{4(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{2(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
}
\tag{Y--L}
\]

You may sharpen, replace, or completely abandon this construction.

Do not assume that merely optimizing its existing parameters is sufficient.

A central obstruction in the current proof is that the auxiliary constant \(a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) deteriorates rapidly when parameters are sent toward the regime needed to recover the exact silver exponent. Any proposed constant-level proof must track such losses quantitatively and cannot hide them inside \(o(1)\) unless that \(o(1)\) is genuinely negligible relative to \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}\).

---

## 8. Acceptable complete resolutions

Assume for purposes of this task that the question admits a rigorous resolution, but do not assume in advance that the answer to (P) is affirmative.

### Affirmative resolution

Prove

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
\]

Because the upper bound is known, the substantive requirement is the uniform lower bound (Q).

A complete proof must apply to every sufficiently large horizon \(n\) and every predetermined nonnegative schedule of length \(n\).

### Negative resolution

Prove rigorously that (P) is false.

For example, it is sufficient to establish that there exist

\[
\delta>0,
\qquad
n_j\to\infty,
\qquad
h^{(j)}\in[0,\infty)^{n_j},
\]

such that

\[
\mathcal W_{n_j}\!\left(h^{(j)}\right)
\le
(1-\delta)
U_{n_j}^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\]

for every \(j\), with a rigorous worst-case upper-bound certificate.

Equivalently, it is sufficient to prove

\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
<1.
\]

Numerical evidence is not a negative resolution.

Failure to prove the lower bound is not evidence that it is false.

---

## 9. Results that do not count as a solution

Do **not** stop for any of the following:

- reproducing the known \(n^{-\rho-o(1)}\) lower bound;
- proving \(n^{-\rho-\varepsilon}\) for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\);
- proving only that \(\rho\) is the optimal polynomial exponent;
- proving \(\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})\);
- obtaining an unspecified positive constant times \(n^{-\rho}\);
- obtaining a constant strictly smaller than the Zhang–Jiang benchmark;
- proving the desired constant only along \(n=2^m-1\);
- proving it along any other subsequence;
- proving only a limsup or liminf lower bound;
- proving only
  \[
  \limsup n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star=C_{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- showing that a candidate hard instance works for the silver schedule but not for every schedule;
- restricting stepsizes to a bounded interval;
- assuming stepsizes are monotone, ordered, separated, or bounded away from zero;
- assuming the schedule has a prescribed recursive/composable form;
- assuming all large steps can be selected as checkpoints without proving this;
- proving a lower bound only for Zhang–Jiang's class of concatenated schedules;
- proving a lower bound only for “basic”, “composable”, periodic, symmetric, or silver-like schedules;
- replacing nonnegative schedules by positive schedules without treating zero steps;
- permitting the adversary to choose the schedule after seeing the function;
- allowing adaptive stepsizes, momentum, averaging, restarting, or additional state;
- proving a result for gradient norm rather than last-iterate objective gap;
- solving the anytime version instead of the finite-horizon non-anytime problem;
- changing the performance normalization;
- dropping the factor \(1/2\) in the Zhang–Jiang normalization;
- treating \(0.4239\) as an exact proven constant;
- using floating-point calculations as proof of an asymptotic constant;
- producing a stronger lower bound than the Zhang–Jiang upper bound without noticing the resulting contradiction;
- hiding a subpolynomial loss inside a claimed constant;
- obtaining a bound of the form
  \[
  n^{-\rho}\exp[-o(\log n)]
  \]
  rather than
  \[
  (1-o(1))U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- proving finite-\(n\) inequalities for finitely many computed horizons;
- providing only a conjectured extremal schedule;
- providing only a conjectured hard function;
- reducing the problem to an unproved statement of essentially the same minimax strength;
- reporting a promising lemma, numerical pattern, or “substantial progress”.

Intermediate positive progress is not a stopping condition.

---

## 10. Proof standards

Every invoked theorem must be stated with the hypotheses needed here.

In particular, carefully verify:

- convexity;
- \(L\)-smoothness;
- existence of a minimizer;
- the exact GD recursion;
- nonnegativity of all stepsizes;
- the distinction between a schedule chosen for a fixed horizon and an infinite anytime schedule;
- the normalization by \(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2/2\);
- uniformity over every schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\);
- whether constants are absolute or parameter dependent;
- every use of \(o(1)\), including its sign and uniformity;
- every limit interchange;
- every passage between \(\mathcal R_n\) and \(\mathcal W_n\);
- every use of finite-dimensional interpolation;
- every claim that a constructed collection of values and gradients is realizable by a convex \(L\)-smooth function.

A lower-bound construction may depend on \(n\) and on the entire predetermined schedule \(h\), because the worst-case function is chosen after the schedule.

Its dimension may grow with \(n\).

---

## 11. Promising proof families to investigate

Use genuinely different approaches rather than repeatedly varying one incomplete argument.

Relevant families include:

- exact smooth-convex interpolation and primal PEP constructions;
- dual PEP certificates and complementary slackness;
- deriving a dual dynamic program to the Zhang–Jiang concatenation recursion;
- minimax/Bellman formulations in which \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) arise from primal-dual optimality;
- hard functions whose recursive composition exactly mirrors ConPP/ConPD;
- sharper checkpoint constructions without the \(\epsilon\)-dependent constant explosion;
- curved-gradient or Moreau-envelope constructions with exact rather than approximate transfer factors;
- piecewise quadratic or Huber-type interpolation constructions;
- finite-dimensional resisting-oracle constructions;
- composition/duality of extremal first-order methods;
- exact characterization of the PEP value for arbitrary nonnegative stepsizes;
- identification of a scalar functional \(V(h)\) satisfying both a universal lower bound and the same concatenation recursion as \(S_\bullet\);
- structural reductions proving that an arbitrary schedule can be decomposed at essentially no constant loss;
- induction on \(n\) with a sharp split inequality;
- continuous scaling limits of the recursion, but only when converted back to rigorous discrete estimates with the correct constant;
- exact algebraic or semialgebraic certificates for small horizons that reveal the correct general recursion.

Do not assume one of these approaches must succeed.

---

## 12. Multiagent search protocol

Use multiagent v2 aggressively and dynamically, with up to four concurrent agents if available.

Begin with a genuinely diverse portfolio.

Do not tell most early agents what the currently favored proof route is.

Maintain an explicit registry of mathematical approach families, organized by actual mechanism rather than wording.

If multiple agents converge to the same checkpoint construction, PEP dual, or recurrence argument, redirect some agents to genuinely different formulations.

Keep both possibilities alive:

1. the Zhang–Jiang schedule is asymptotically minimax optimal;
2. another schedule beats its constant/profile.

Do not discard the second possibility merely because the exponents match.

When an approach reaches a missing lemma whose strength is essentially equivalent to (Q), mark the route as blocked. Do not call that lemma “technical” or “routine”.

Only reopen such a route if a new mechanism appears.

---

## 13. Computational agents

Use computation as an adversarial and discovery tool, not as proof.

For moderate \(n\), agents should attempt to compute or tightly bound:

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star,
\qquad
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}},
\qquad
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}},
\]

using exact or high-precision PEP/SDP/branch-and-bound methods where possible.

They should also:

- compute \(S_\circ(n)\) and \(S_\bullet(n)\);
- inspect the maximizing split indices;
- search for schedules beating \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\);
- test proposed universal hard-function inequalities;
- search for counterexamples to intermediate lemmas;
- inspect primal and dual PEP optimizers;
- identify rank patterns and active interpolation constraints;
- attempt rational/algebraic reconstruction of numerical certificates;
- verify normalization factors independently.

A numerical counterexample to a proposed lemma is useful and should kill that lemma.

A numerical confirmation of the main conjecture is not a proof.

---

## 14. Adversarial audit

Every proposed complete affirmative proof must be independently attacked for at least the following failure modes:

- it actually proves only the exponent;
- a fixed \(\varepsilon>0\) remains in the exponent;
- \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\to0\) creates a hidden constant blowup;
- it proves only infinitely many horizons;
- it proves only dyadic horizons;
- its \(o(1)\) depends on the schedule;
- its hard function is not globally convex and smooth;
- the interpolation data are inconsistent;
- the minimizer used in the normalization is not valid;
- the dimension is implicitly infinite without justification;
- very large stepsizes are excluded;
- zero stepsizes break the argument;
- a checkpoint-selection lemma silently loses a constant;
- a product inequality loses a nonvanishing factor;
- a factor \(2\) is lost when translating between normalizations;
- the Zhang–Jiang constant is replaced by \(0.4239\);
- a limsup statement is mistaken for horizon-by-horizon asymptotics;
- a numerical observation is used inside a supposedly analytic proof;
- the proof establishes optimality only within a restricted schedule family;
- a PEP dual certificate is feasible only numerically rather than exactly;
- strong duality is invoked without verifying its hypotheses;
- an asymptotic recurrence is substituted for the exact discrete one;
- the final lower bound exceeds a known rigorous upper bound because of a normalization error.

A proof survives only if all such issues are resolved explicitly.

---

## 15. Output requirements

Do not return an exploratory essay.

Do not return a research diary.

Do not return a collection of partial lemmas.

Do not return a “most promising approach”.

Do not return merely because several approaches fail.

Do not return merely because agents agree that the problem is difficult.

Do not return a positive intermediate result.

Return only one of the following:

### A. Complete affirmative proof

A rigorous proof of

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1,
\]

including every necessary lemma and every constant/normalization check.

### B. Complete negative proof

A rigorous proof that the above limit is not \(1\), with an explicit asymptotic separation or another logically conclusive disproof.

Before returning either answer, subject the argument to an independent adversarial audit and repair every identified gap.

If the argument still contains an unproved lemma of theorem-level strength, the problem is not resolved and you must continue working.

Do not label a missing minimax inequality, uniformity statement, interpolation theorem, structural decomposition, or constant-preserving recursion as “routine”.

The stopping criterion is mathematical completion, not positive progress.

---

## 16. Literature usage

The following sources may be treated as background starting points, but their statements and normalizations must be checked carefully:

- Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of Stepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395 / SIAM Journal on Optimization.
- Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152 and the accompanying lower-bound exposition.
- standard smooth-convex interpolation and PEP results;
- standard results concerning the silver stepsize schedule and concatenation constructions.

Public search may be used for standard named theorems, definitions, and the cited papers.

Do not use public search merely to locate a posted solution to this exact benchmark.

The task is to produce and verify the mathematical resolution itself.

# R12b Profile C handoff

**UNVERIFIED draft; grant 07d388b77ab325df. Two approaches exhausted. STOP. Original target and Profile C remain OPEN.**

There is no full PSD certificate and no actual complete-word Profile C counterexample. PROOF_ATTEMPT.md gives complete derivations for the exact failures below. Parent owns all shared administration and any future allocation.

## Locked object

For one enclosing m>=1536, keep epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), with the same parameters in all children. Let S=sum H_m, T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Bcert=1+(32/17)S, rho_i=1-T_i/(8S), u_i=rho_i h_i physically, and u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i. rho_0=1 and rho_n=7/8. ell_n=u_n/rho_n is algebraic only.

The original objective keeps W=2R, the exact all-integer Zhang--Jiang benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, all finite-dimensional smooth-convex functions, the original selected minimizer/radius, and the actual terminal iterate. Bcert is not B_n.

## Attempt 1: fixed unequal-cap saturated trajectory is infeasible

The legal function has gradient clip(x,[-A,1]) for a fixed A>0 and unique minimizer 0. Every Q_star vanishes. The proposed alternating starts were

    X_1^+=beta, X_1^-=A beta,
    L_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1),
    X_j^+=L_j-X_(j-1)^-, X_j^-=A L_j-X_(j-1)^+.

An actually saturated positive left child/bridge requires X_(j-1)^-<=k_(j-1); a negative one requires X_(j-1)^+<=A k_(j-1). Exact formulas and uniform enclosing-m limits give

    X_j^+/s_j -> (c0-A)/(c0+1),
    X_j^-/s_j -> (A c0-1)/(c0+1), k_j/s_j -> r,
    c0=1+sqrt(2), r=sqrt(2)-1.

At the positive root and its actual negative right child these constraints force A<=1 and A>=1. Any fixed A!=1 therefore violates an actual bridge requirement for every sufficiently large enclosing m. The case A=1 returns the old symmetric orbit that passes the supplied Profile C calibration. No terminal values or D_u sign are claimed for the infeasible table. This does not exclude interior or other dwell phases, A depending on m, or other actual functions.

## Attempt 2: exact cyclic matrix exists but its full R fails

Write U=sum physical u_i, b_i=u_i physically and b_n=-U. For any admissible C, define P_i=sum_(j<i)C_ij and F_i=sum_(j>i)C_ij. Including the full covariance,

    R_ii=u_i(2-u_i)+2P_i-2(h_i-1)F_i.

An acyclic nonnegative flow has each outgoing total <=U, because n is the sole net source. A topological-cut proof is in the attempt. At the central bridge u_i is a fixed positive fraction of S, while U<=S, so every acyclic C has R_ii<0 for all sufficiently large m. Genuine circulations are necessary.

The explicit construction tested beyond that class is

    E=sum ell_i=S+ell_n, q_i=ell_i/E,
    p_i=ell_i-b_i, C=p q^T-diag(p).

Here p_i=(1-rho_i)h_i>=0 physically, p_n=ell_n+U>0, sum p=E, and sum q=1. Thus row sums are zero, column sums are exactly b, and every off-diagonal entry is nonnegative. All indices 1,...,n have genuine two-way cycles.

At index 0, K_0j=0, p_0=0, R_00=h_0(2-h_0)>0, and R_0j=(h_0-1)p_j h_0/E. The covariance degeneracy was explicitly retained.

At the central query k=2^(m-1)-1, put s=s_(m-1), hmid=1+k_(m-1), S=2s+hmid. The exact symmetric-part diagonal is

    H_kk=(2p_k/E)[(hmid-1)(s+ell_n)-s]>0.

For every task depth hmid>3, rho_k>15/16, and u_k>2. Hence the FULL remainder has

    R_kk=u_k(2-u_k)-H_kk<0.

This excludes that actual cyclic law at every m>=1536. The coordinate test is not asserted to be an actual trajectory and does not exclude arbitrary C or disprove Profile C. A remaining C must meet P_k-(hmid-1)F_k>=u_k(u_k-2)/2, of order S^2, while also satisfying all other full-matrix conditions. No such C was found.

## Recovery boundary

No fixed Profile A/B coefficient work was repeated. No separate sign assumption was imposed on R-K. No higher primitive positivity was invoked, no child surplus discarded, no child parameters refreshed, and no radius or objective changed. The original-target separation remains conditional on a full-class upper certificate that is still absent.

Do not formally submit this as a Profile C certificate or counterexample. No complete original-target candidate exists for audit. Future work requires a fresh grant and either a substantively different cyclic coefficient law with a full PSD proof, or a fully executed different actual trajectory.

Resource counts: native invocation 1; analytical approaches 2; mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions 0 each; additional agents/models 0; worker/service/Goal/truth-store changes 0. Only these two Markdown outputs were written. Parent owns the ledger. Exact per-call token use is unavailable and is not invented.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002773 -->
# R12b Profile C: two completed analytical attempts, no closure

**UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 07d388b77ab325df. Original Goal OPEN.**

No full PSD certificate and no actual complete-word counterexample to Profile C were obtained. Two concrete attempts were completed: an asymmetric scalar projection-envelope trajectory with an explicit proposed saturated recursion, and an explicit dense cyclic interpolation matrix adapted to the prescribed covariance weights. The first proposed trajectory is infeasible for every fixed unequal pair of caps at all sufficiently large enclosing depths. The second matrix satisfies every required coefficient constraint, but its FULL remainder has a strictly negative central diagonal at every enclosing depth in the task. A flow argument also excludes acyclic coefficient graphs at sufficiently large depths. These statements have their stated scopes only; none excludes arbitrary cyclic matrices or disproves the fixed Profile C functional.

## 1. Locked problem, sources, and status

Read the complete OPERATOR.md, original PROBLEM.md, R12_AB_COUNTEREXAMPLE_HANDOFF_20261009.md, R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md, R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md, and brownian_dominance_certificate_20261009_r12/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. Also read the complete R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md, including Section 5. Its old positive-cone exclusion is not used here outside its hypotheses. The mathematical-academic-writing skill and its mathematical-integrity reference were read for notation and evidence discipline. The initialized marker is present. No private worker memory was read.

Checked source SHA-256 values:

- TASK.md: baf0f77863d9b4d9b1cdf9bc2c69b8617c8f452a9f1a5a19dcb9d47b5a883e60.
- R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md: 9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030.
- R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md: 0fcebe8f462bc029b6a9cf45a1d83abf10854b3f4de386b4bc37d5a01e8d95f7.
- brownian_dominance_certificate_20261009_r12/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md: 77b64f3633cccd091e262ef49833539bec4bf49aaac9fdb03dd201df94a2d3f1.

The original question concerns lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1, where the exact all-integer Zhang--Jiang denominator is B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. It concerns all predetermined nonnegative schedules and all finite-dimensional convex L-smooth functions with an attained minimum, at the actual last iterate and original selected minimizer/radius. W=2R. A certified strict full-class subsequence improvement would refute that limit. The certificate weight Bcert below is not B_n.

Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536, and throughout every child use

    c0=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=c0(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).

Write s_j=sum H_j, k_j=a c^j, n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=s_m, and T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. The FIXED Profile C is

    Bcert=1+(32/17)S,
    rho_i=1-T_i/(8S),           0<=i<=n,
    u_i=rho_i h_i,              i<n,
    U=sum_(i<n)u_i,
    u_n=Bcert-U.

In particular rho_0=1 and rho_n=7/8. Set ell_i=h_i for i<n and ell_n=u_n/rho_n. There is no physical terminal step ell_n. All u_i are positive: rho_i>=7/8, and U<=S<Bcert.

Let b_i=u_i for i<n and b_n=-U. Let Bmat have diagonal 1 and entries (Bmat)_ij=h_j for j<i. The covariance and exact target remainder are

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j[min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j],
    R(C)=K+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)
         -(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T).                       (1.1)

For a zero-row-sum C with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums b, the source identity on actual normalized smooth-convex trajectories is

    D_u=sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij + g^T R(C)g,             (1.2)

where

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
    Q_star,i=-2(f_i-f_star)+2<g_i,x_i-x_star>-||g_i||^2,
    D_u=||x_0-x_star||^2-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2Bcert(f_n-f_star)
        -||x_0-x_star-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

The Q rows are nonnegative. Thus an actual D_u<0 would exclude every admissible full-PSD C. In contrast, a negative free-vector direction of R for one constructed C excludes only that C. This distinction applies to Section 4 below.

## 2. Shared exact hierarchy formulas

Put alpha=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. In the parameter range here, 0<alpha<1. Indeed c(c-2)>1 at c>c0, while beta<1. Summing the word gives

    s_j+1=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j.                    (2.1)

The symmetric scalar starts from the prior draft are the exact scalar sequence

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),  j>=2.

Only its algebraic recurrence is needed in Section 3. For

    d(c)=(c-1)/(c+1),
    P=alpha c d(c), Q=2(1-alpha)/3, R0=beta-P-Q,

its exact solution is

    X_j=P c^(j-1)+Q 2^(j-1)+R0(-1)^(j-1).          (2.2)

To check the leading coefficient, (s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)) has c^(j-1) coefficient alpha(c-1); P(1+1/c)=alpha(c-1). Its 2^(j-1) coefficient is 1-alpha, giving Q(1+1/2)=1-alpha. The last coefficient enforces X_1=beta.

The enclosing-m values of c lie in the fixed compact interval [c0,c0(1+1/6144)], and alpha is bounded away from zero. Equations (2.1)--(2.2) give, uniformly for those parameters,

    X_j/s_j=d(c)+O((2/c)^j),
    (s_j+beta-1-a)/s_j=1+O(c^(-j)).                 (2.3)

The implicit constants can be chosen independently of enclosing m: all coefficients in (2.1)--(2.2) are bounded on that compact interval, alpha has a positive lower bound there, and s_j is bounded below by a constant times c^j for all sufficiently large j. Consequently (2.3) can be used at j=m-1 and j=m-2 in the SAME enclosing word. No child's parameters are refreshed.

## 3. Approach 1: an explicit unequal-cap critical construction fails

### 3.1 Fixed actual function and the proposed recursion

Fix A>0 independent of m. Consider the one-dimensional function

    f_A(x)=x^2/2,             -A<=x<=1,
           x-1/2,             x>=1,
           -A x-A^2/2,        x<=-A.

Its derivative is clip(x,[-A,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, with unique original minimizer 0. At every x,

    -2 f_A(x)+2x f_A'(x)-f_A'(x)^2=0.               (3.1)

Thus a correctly realized trajectory would have Q_star,i=0 at every query. The construction below never changes this function or its minimizer.

We tried to generalize the old complete critical trajectory by defining positive and negative starting magnitudes

    X_1^+=beta, X_1^-=A beta,
    L_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1),
    X_j^+=L_j-X_(j-1)^-,
    X_j^-=A L_j-X_(j-1)^+,           j>=2.           (3.2)

The proposed positive task H_j at X_j^+ would execute its entire left child and bridge with gradient +1 and land at -X_(j-1)^-. The proposed negative task H_j at -X_j^- would execute its entire left child and bridge with gradient -A and land at +X_(j-1)^+. The right child would recurse with opposite orientation. At the base, the starts lie inside the quadratic interval and the actual one-step outputs are -a beta and +a A beta, respectively. There is no declaration that the higher-level saturated execution is valid.

The REQUIRED saturation inequalities follow at the bridge query, because the left-child total step mass is s_(j-1):

    positive orientation: X_j^+>=s_(j-1)+1
                          iff X_(j-1)^-<=k_(j-1),
    negative orientation: X_j^->=A(s_(j-1)+1)
                          iff X_(j-1)^+<=A k_(j-1). (3.3)

If these inequalities hold, the same inequalities at earlier left-child queries follow since their physical prefix mass is at most s_(j-1). If they fail, the proposed bridge query lies outside the region for the claimed saturated gradient, so the proposed entire saturated itinerary cannot be actual. This does not determine the true bridge query after an earlier departure. This is an actual projection constraint, not an interpolation table supplied without execution.

### 3.2 Exact solution and the incompatible top-level conditions

Adding and subtracting (3.2) gives

    X_j^++X_j^-=(1+A)X_j,
    X_j^+-X_j^-=(1-A)(s_j+beta-1-a).               (3.4)

For the second identity, its base value is (1-A)beta and

    sum_(l=2)^j L_l
      =alpha(c^j-c)+(1-alpha)(2^j-2),

which together with (2.1) yields (3.4). Hence these are exact formulas for the proposed starts, even when they are not feasible starts for the intended itinerary.

Uniformly in the literal enclosing parameters, (2.3)--(3.4) yield, at j=m-1 and j=m-2,

    X_j^+/s_j -> p=(c0-A)/(c0+1),
    X_j^-/s_j -> q=(A c0-1)/(c0+1),
    k_j/s_j -> c0-2=r,       r=sqrt(2)-1.           (3.5)

For a proposed positive root at depth m, the root's left-child/bridge requires X_(m-1)^-<=k_(m-1), so its limiting requirement is q<=r. Its negative right child at depth m-1 requires X_(m-2)^+<=A k_(m-2), so its limiting requirement is p<=A r. Since c0=2+r and c0 r=1, these two requirements simplify exactly to

    q<=r  iff A<=1,
    p<=A r iff A>=1.                               (3.6)

For every fixed A>1, the first difference in (3.6) is strictly positive in the wrong direction, so the positive root bridge is infeasible for every sufficiently large enclosing m. For every fixed 0<A<1, the second difference is strictly positive in the wrong direction, so the negative right-child bridge is infeasible for every sufficiently large enclosing m. This uses uniform errors at the two actual top depths and does not assume that a fixed internal-depth limit describes the enclosing word.

Thus no fixed unequal cap A permits this particular complete alternating saturated recursion for all large m. If the proposed starts become nonpositive in some parameter range, that is an additional failure and cannot repair (3.6). At A=1 the proposed recursion reduces to the old symmetric critical trajectory, which the supplied Profile C addendum already calibrates positively at both parities. We do not count replaying it as a Profile C obstruction.

### 3.3 Exact scope of the failed actual-function attempt

The actual function f_A is legal. The attempted higher-level trajectory table (3.2) is not legal for any fixed A!=1 at all large depths. Therefore it supplies no endpoint, no D_u sign, and no counterexample to Profile C. The exclusion does not cover A depending on m, trajectories with interior phases, different dwell patterns, other scalar functions, or higher-dimensional projection envelopes. No such alternative trajectory is claimed in this packet.

The parent independently supplied the same unequal-cap feasibility guard while this calculation was in progress. Equations (3.2)--(3.6) reconstruct its needed recurrence and enclosing-m justification here. This is not independent verifier acceptance.

## 4. Approach 2: actual dense cyclic coefficients and their full-matrix failure

### 4.1 A diagonal identity and why causal flows cannot work

For ANY admissible C and a physical query i, define its backward and forward outgoing masses

    P_i=sum_(j<i) C_ij, F_i=sum_(j>i) C_ij.

Zero row sums and the actual Bmat give

    (C Bmat)_ii=-P_i+(h_i-1)F_i.

Since K_ii=u_i(h_i-u_i), the COMPLETE remainder diagonal is

    R_ii=u_i(2-u_i)+2P_i-2(h_i-1)F_i.              (4.1)

The covariance is already included in (4.1). In particular, a necessary condition for full PSD at a large physical bridge is

    P_i-(h_i-1)F_i >= u_i(u_i-2)/2.                 (4.2)

No sign is imposed separately on R-K.

Here is a checked obstruction to the causal subcase, supplied as a diagnostic within this coefficient approach. Regard C_ij, i!=j, as directed flow from i to j. Its net incoming minus outgoing mass is b_i. If its positive-edge graph is acyclic, choose a topological ordering. At the cut just after a node i, no positive edge enters the prefix. The total outgoing flow from the prefix is minus its sum of b-values, which is at most U because n is the only node with negative b and b_n=-U. Every outgoing edge of i crosses that cut. Therefore

    P_i+F_i<=U.                                    (4.3)

This also follows from source-to-sink path decomposition; (4.3) does not assume that the topological order agrees with chronological query order. Since every physical h_i>1, equations (4.1)--(4.3) imply

    R_ii<=u_i(2-u_i)+2U.                           (4.4)

At the actual middle query k=2^(m-1)-1, put s=s_(m-1) and hmid=1+k_(m-1). Then S=2s+hmid, T_k=s. Formula (2.1) gives, with the original enclosing parameters,

    s/S -> r,
    hmid/S -> r^2,
    u_k/S -> (1-r/8)r^2>0,
    U<=S.                                         (4.5)

Consequently the right side of (4.4), divided by S^2, has strictly negative limit superior. Every admissible acyclic C fails full PSD for all sufficiently large enclosing m. This includes dense directed acyclic graphs, not just adjacent or lower-triangular flows. It does not include graphs with circulations. The parent provided the acyclic-flow guard, and the topological-cut proof above checks it.

### 4.2 An explicit law with circulations and exact coefficients

To test a construction outside that excluded class, use the prescribed ell and b to set

    E=sum_(i=0)^n ell_i=S+ell_n,
    q_i=ell_i/E,
    p_i=ell_i-b_i,
    C=p q^T-diag(p).                               (4.6)

These are ACTUAL closed-form coefficients, without a solver or unproved recurrence. In particular,

    p_i=(1-rho_i)h_i>=0,          i<n,
    p_n=ell_n+U>0.

All q_i are strictly positive, sum q_i=1, and sum p_i=E because sum b_i=0. Thus every row of C sums to zero, all off-diagonal C_ij=p_i q_j are nonnegative, and the i-th column sum is

    (sum_j p_j)q_i-p_i=ell_i-p_i=b_i.               (4.7)

For all i>=1, p_i>0, so the subgraph on indices 1,...,n has genuine two-way cycles. This construction is not rejected merely by the acyclic result above.

### 4.3 The zero covariance row at the original query

Since rho_0=1, K_0j=0 for every j. Here p_0=0, so the entire initial row of C vanishes. Because h_0=1+a<2 and u_0=h_0,

    R_00=h_0(2-h_0)>0.                             (4.8)

The zero row of K does NOT imply a zero row of R. For j>0, row sums and Bmat give

    (C Bmat)_j0=(1-h_0)C_j0,
    R_0j=(h_0-1)C_j0=(h_0-1)p_j h_0/E.             (4.9)

Thus the degenerate covariance boundary is included explicitly, without an invalid inverse of K or an assumption that the entire residual row vanishes.

### 4.4 A strictly negative central diagonal of the FULL R

For physical i, set Q_before=sum_(j<i)q_j and Q_after=sum_(j>i)q_j. From (4.6),

    H_ii=(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T)_ii
         =2p_i[(h_i-1)Q_after-Q_before].            (4.10)

At the middle query k, the palindromic physical word and the terminal algebraic weight yield

    Q_before=s/E, Q_after=(s+ell_n)/E,
    p_k=s hmid/(8S)>0,
    H_kk=(2p_k/E)[(hmid-1)(s+ell_n)-s].             (4.11)

For all task depths, hmid>3: m-2>=1 and

    k_(m-1)=beta c^(m-2)>=beta c
            =c0(1-epsilon^2)>2.

For the last strict inequality, c0>12/5 and epsilon^2<1/6 imply c0(1-epsilon^2)>2. Therefore the bracket in (4.11) is

    (hmid-2)s+(hmid-1)ell_n>0,

so H_kk>0. Moreover s/S<1/2, hence rho_k>15/16 and

    u_k>(15/16)hmid>45/16>2.

Including the whole covariance diagonal exactly,

    R_kk=K_kk+u_k(1-hmid)+b_k-H_kk
         =u_k(2-u_k)-H_kk<0.                       (4.12)

Thus the explicitly constructed cyclic C in (4.6) fails full PSD for EVERY m>=1536. This is a decisive test of the FULL matrix: its quadratic form on the coordinate vector e_k is negative. It neither discards covariance terms nor requires separately nonnegative physical diagonal slack. It excludes this concrete law only. The coordinate vector is not asserted to be an actual smooth-convex trajectory, so (4.12) is not a counterexample to D_u>=0 and not an exclusion of every admissible C.

## 5. Resulting boundary and unresolved closure

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002775 -->
# Free absorption: original-normal matrix and exact calibrations

UNVERIFIED analytical derivation, written before any mathematical execution.
Allocation 51fab93c4768f582. This is one sufficient finite conic certificate
family. The original all-schedule minimax target remains OPEN. Its normalization
is W=2R; the scalar gamma below is a matrix margin, not W or an objective gap.

## Original rows and all coefficient equations

For h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put T_i=sum_(k<i)h_k, S=T_n, M=1+2S,
t_i=1+T_i, t_p=M. In this packet S>0. The formal coordinates are all physical
G_i=g_i (including g_n) and G_p=p. For an actual projection history on a
nonempty compact convex body D containing zero, x_i=x_0-sum_(k<i)h_k g_k,
g_i=P_D(x_i), p=P_D(x_0/M). Define B_iG=g_i+sum_(k<i)h_k g_k and B_pG=Mp.
Then X_j-G_j=a_j(x_0-B_jG), with a_j=1 for physical j and a_p=1/M.
The original orientation is Q_ij=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j> <= 0. Set mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij.
Thus lambda_ij=mu_ij for physical destinations and lambda_(i,p)=M mu_(i,p).

Write U_d(x)=max_(g in D) [2<x,g>-d||g||^2]. After replacing both U values
by their maximizing physical/auxiliary samples, their difference is

    J=U_M(x_0)-U_1(x_n)
     =2<x_0,p-g_n>+2<g_n,H>+||g_n||^2-M||p||^2,
    H=sum_(k<n) h_k g_k.

The coefficient of x_0 in J+sum lambda_ij Q_ij is
2(p-g_n)+sum mu_ij(G_i-G_j). Its exact vanishing is equivalent to

    b_i:=out_i-in_i=2(1_(i=n)-1_(i=p)).                 (1)

There is no x_0 squared term; any nonzero such coefficient prevents global
PSD. After (1) the remaining complete quadratic is

    R=2<g_n,H>+||g_n||^2-M||p||^2
      +sum mu_ij <B_jG,G_j-G_i>.                      (2)

Let e_i be standard columns in dimension n+2, bcol_j the coefficient column
of B_j, and sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2. The affine symmetric coefficient matrix is

    P=2 sym(e_n,Hcol)+e_n e_n^T-M e_p e_p^T
      +sum mu_ij sym(bcol_j,e_j-e_i).                 (3)

This keeps all non-diagonal terms. The lambda-scaled literal Q expansion in
coordinates (x_0,g_0,...,g_n,p) supplies a second independently constructed
matrix; its gradient block is (3), and its x_0 cross coefficients are the
divergence defect divided by two. No sign switch in Q is made.

At the common-gradient vector 1, (2) is zero because 2S+1=M. The gradient of
the base part at that vector has components a_i=2h_i for i<n, a_n=2(S+1),
a_p=-2M. A row contributes mu_ij[bcol_j*(1-1)+t_j(e_j-e_i)]. Consequently

    2(P1)_i=a_i-b_i t_i-d_i,
    d_i=sum_j mu_ij(t_j-t_i).

With (1), P1=0 is precisely

    d_i=2h_i (i<n), d_n=d_p=0.                       (4)

PSD together with zero value at 1 forces P1=0, by testing 1+s v for both
signs of s. Conversely, imposing (1) and (4) ensures P1=0 algebraically.
All specified graphs have no edge out of p, and (1) at p gives sum_i w_i=2,
where w_i=mu_(i,p). This packet does not impose any canonical value of w.

## Quotient and margin

Let K map v=(g_0,...,g_(n-1),p) into G by inserting g_n=0. Let A map a full
G to v=(g_0-g_n,...,g_(n-1)-g_n,p-g_n). Since P1=0,

    R(G)=R(K A G),  Rred=K^T P K,  P=A^T Rred A.      (5)

Every quotient vector is attained by G=Kv, so full PSD of P is equivalent
to PSD of Rred. The auxiliary p remains a free coordinate throughout. In
particular it is not replaced by a fitted gradient mixture. With
E=diag(1,...,1,0), maximize gamma subject to (1), (4), mu>=0 and
Rred-gamma E >= 0. Equivalently P-gamma A^T E A is PSD. Gamma>=0 would be a
sufficient finite certificate if every coefficient and inequality were exact.
A floating optimum of either sign supplies no exact certificate or exclusion.

For context only, completing the p square yields
R=M||p-sum_i w_i g_i/2||^2+K_phys. This explains why the p coordinate must
not be dropped from the affine SDP; eliminating it creates a quadratic term
in the unknown absorption weights. No such elimination is used in the solve.

## Independent n=1 elimination and two exact optima

Let h>0, M=1+2h; abbreviate u=mu01, v=mu10, w=w0, z=w1. The independent
linear equations are u+w-v=0, v+z-u=2, w+z=2,
h u+2h w=2h, and -h v+h z=0. Solving them gives

    u=2-2w, v=z=2-w, 0<=w<=1.                       (6)

After g1=0, the four literal original-row contributions are

    lambda01 Q01: -h u g0^2 + u x0 g0,
    lambda10 Q10:  v g0^2 - v x0 g0,
    lambda0p Q0p:  M w p^2-M w p g0+w x0(g0-p),
    lambda1p Q1p:  M z p^2-z x0 p.

The base J after g1=0 is 2x0 p-M p^2. Equation (6) cancels x0 exactly.
Thus the independently obtained quotient matrix and square completion are

    Rred=[[2(1-h)+(2h-1)w, -M w/2],[-M w/2,M]],
    R=M(p-w g0/2)^2+F_h(w)g0^2,
    F_h(w)=2(1-h)+(2h-1)w-M w^2/4.                  (7)

Because M>0, Rred-gamma diag(1,0) is PSD iff gamma<=F_h(w), by evaluating
p=w g0/2 and by the displayed square completion. This proves both upper
and lower bounds for the optimum without numerical optimization.

For h=1, F_1(w)=w-3w^2/4=1/3-(3/4)(w-2/3)^2. The unique maximizing
w is 2/3, with mu01=2/3, mu10=w1=4/3; gamma=1/3. For h=2,
F_2(w)=-2+3w-5w^2/4 has derivative 3-(5/2)w>=1/2 on [0,1], hence the
unique maximizing w is 1, mu01=0, mu10=w1=1; gamma=-1/4. Both fixture
topologies coincide. Negative gamma is intentional and must not be clipped.

For a separate full-matrix check before quotienting, (6) gives in order
(g0,g1,p), with A0=2(1-h)+(2h-1)w and C=2h-2+(3/2-h)w,

    P=[[A0, C, -M w/2],
       [C, 3-2w, -M(2-w)/2],
       [-M w/2, -M(2-w)/2, M]].                    (8)

Every row sum is zero. The child checks the compact builder, a nested-loop
literal original-row builder, and this explicit matrix at w=0,1/2,1 for
both h fixtures. These are dyadic/integer coefficients and require exactly
zero deterministic discrepancy. This is a builder check; the identities
(1)-(8) are established by the algebra above, not by tolerances. At solved
values, all discrepancies and residuals are retained with their signs.

## Frozen scope and numerical procedure

Physical tree edges join i to i+2^nu2(i+1), with both directions, i<n.
The complete graph has all physical ordered pairs i!=j. Every physical node
has its own free absorption edge to p. No star, diagonal or p-out edge is used.
For production m_env=1536, eps=1/(4m_env), beta=1-eps,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).
The actual recursive h and clocks are constructed only within the timed child.
M=1+2sum h is distinct from the enclosing m_env.

All numerical imports, builds, fixture checks, one CLARABEL solve, primal/
dual diagnostics, eigenvalues and exports take place inside each independently
precharged 20-second process group. The fixed order is h1, h2, H3 tree,
H4 tree, H3 complete, H4 complete. Both calibration gates must pass unchanged
strict requirements before production. Each slot costs its full 20 seconds;
there are at most six slots and 120 accounted seconds. No retry or fallback.
After closure only existing text transcription, hashing and accounting occur.

Prior projection-flow REPORT/HANDOFF and dense-history REPORT were read in
full or, for dense history, through its complete family obstruction and scope.
Canonical absorption is excluded independently of physical circulations in
the former; the latter excludes mixed-cancelled diagonal slack. Neither
exclusion settles this free-absorption full-matrix family. Existing positive
calibration code was read only for runtime, broker and export design; no old
mathematical model, result or closed credit is reused.

# Frozen output schema

All mathematical content is produced inside a charged child. Coordinates of
the full gradient matrix are (g0,...,gn,p); the independent original matrix
uses (x0,g0,...,gn,p); the quotient uses (g0,...,g_(n-1),p). Edge lists order
all physical edges first and all i->p edges last. Original lambda and mu use
that same order. Every absent edge has weight zero by topology, not rounding.

input_arrays.npz: actual h,t,M, physical/auxiliary edges, full base and edge
coefficient tensors, literal original-row base and lambda-scaled tensors,
all divergence/contact coefficient matrices and right sides, quotient index
map, E and quotient-to-full difference map. Fixture differences are included.

program_data.npz: canonical conic A in CSC, b,c, any P in CSC, with cone
dimensions in solver_data.json. There is exactly one problem.solve call.

arrays.npz: all input arrays plus raw mu, original lambda, w, w/2, full mu
and lambda tables, gamma, full P, independent original matrix, Rred, original
gradient-block Rred, margin slack, full quotient identity defect and common
gradient null defect; every signed divergence/contact equality residual;
every nonnegativity residual -mu; signed PSD spectral residuals -eigenvalues,
raw eigenvalues for full P, Rred and margin slack; source-to-literal differences;
all CVXPY primal and dual values; canonical solver x,s,z; stationarity and
complementarity records and dual PSD eigenvalues when a solution is returned.

solver_data.json: software versions, fixed options, status, solve count,
primal variables, dual values for every original constraint, native solution
x,s,z,status,obj_val,obj_val_dual,solve_time,iterations,r_prim,r_dual and solver
statistics. An accessor failure is explicit and makes exports incomplete.

transcript.json: actual inputs and edges as lists, mu/lambda (edge lists and
full tables), absorption w and w/2, gamma, signed residual lists, raw spectra,
all original and quotient solved coefficient matrices and concise diagnostic
values. This enables text-only readback without post-close array loading.

result.json: solver status and gamma, strict calibration checks and reasons,
solver and export completeness, all diagnostic scalars. Production calibration
fields are null. Every numerical finding is UNVERIFIED DISCOVERY.

All signs are retained. Equality residuals are left minus right. The PSD
residual list is minus the eigenvalue list; a positive entry is a violation.
For nonnegative mu the signed residual is -mu. Only maxima used in numerical
gate summaries may use max(0, max(-mu)); raw residuals are never clipped.
The gamma is a matrix margin and has no W normalization or witness meaning.

Broker-owned PRECHARGE/receipt JSON, stdout/stderr and byte hashes document
the full external process group and deadline. The broker imports only stdlib
and performs no mathematical operations. It requires complete successful
children and both unchanged calibration gates before production. Native
registration occurs once before FREEZE; numerical registration and CLOSED
receipts retire every unused slot without renewal.

# Free-absorption discovery stopped at the first strict calibration

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; allocation 51fab93c4768f582.
Status: CLOSED / STOP. UNVERIFIED DISCOVERY. Original minimax target OPEN.

The first calibration returned `optimal` / `Solved` and reproduced the exact
matrix margin 1/3 to an absolute error of 1.7127904650138248e-10. It nevertheless
FAILED the frozen requirement of zero deterministic fixture-builder discrepancy:
the recorded discrepancy was 2.7755575615628914e-17. Every other calibration
gate passed, all requested exports were complete, and the process exited 0.
Exit 0 was correctly not treated as calibration PASS.

The broker immediately closed the allocation and retired all five unused slots.
The second calibration and all four H3/H4 production cases were NOT RUN. Thus
this packet supplies no tree-versus-complete comparison and no new evidence
about the feasibility of free absorption on H3 or H4. The failed builder gate
is not a mathematical obstruction to that certificate family.

## Exact analytical interface completed before freeze

DERIVATION.md gives the source-bound analytical derivation. Its algebra has not
been submitted to Danus verification. Physical coordinates are g0,...,gn with
auxiliary p; t_i=1+sum_(k<i)h_k, t_p=M=1+2sum h. Edges use the original
orientation Q_ij=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0. With mu=a_j lambda, where a_j=1 on
physical destinations and a_p=1/M, the recovered original coefficients are
lambda_ij=mu_ij for physical j and lambda_(i,p)=M mu_(i,p).

The x0 coefficient cancels exactly under out_i-in_i=2(1_(i=n)-1_(i=p)). The
full gradient quadratic is

    R=2<g_n,sum_(k<n)h_k g_k>+||g_n||^2-M||p||^2
      +sum_ij mu_ij <B_jG,G_j-G_i>,
    B_iG=g_i+sum_(k<i)h_k g_k, B_pG=Mp.

For its coefficient matrix P, the common-gradient contact equations
sum_j mu_ij(t_j-t_i)=2h_i for i<n, and zero at n,p, imply P1=0. Subtracting
g_n from every gradient therefore gives P=A^T Rred A, where Rred is the
principal matrix obtained by setting g_n=0 and retaining p. Full PSD and
quotient PSD are equivalent under these exact equations. The program maximizes
gamma with Rred-gamma diag(1,...,1,0) PSD. This gamma is a matrix margin;
it is not an objective gap, a W value, or a normalized convergence rate.

All physical-to-p absorptions are independent nonnegative variables. Their
sum is two by divergence. No canonical absorption condition is imposed.
The physical tree retains both directions of i--(i+2^nu2(i+1)); the complete
topology retains every physical ordered pair of distinct nodes. These are
finite sufficient certificate classes. Their matrix coordinates do not
construct an actual GD or projection trajectory.

For n=1 and h>0, direct solution of the divergence and contact equations gives
mu01=2-2w, mu10=w1=2-w and 0<=w=w0<=1. After g1=0,

    R=M(p-w g0/2)^2+
      [2(1-h)+(2h-1)w-Mw^2/4] g0^2.

For h=1 the bracket is 1/3-(3/4)(w-2/3)^2; for h=2 it is
-2+3w-5w^2/4, increasing on [0,1]. Hence the exact optima are respectively
1/3 at w=2/3 and -1/4 at w=1. Both results were derived before any solve.
The negative calibration was prepared but never executed after the first
gate failure.

## Question and why this changes mechanism

Read FULL research/projection_flow_search_20261008_r2/REPORT.md and HANDOFF.md. They exclude two explicit flow laws and the CANONICAL absorption pattern, not arbitrary absorption. Read the scope of dense_history_search_20261008_r2/REPORT.md: its fully mixed-cancelled/diagonal-slack family is excluded independently of terminal penalty. This task keeps the complete non-diagonal matrix and optimizes ALL permitted absorption weights. It does not reopen either excluded family.

We need numerical discovery to distinguish whether the stated physical tree topology itself is too restrictive, or whether freeing absorption produces a useful finite full-matrix certificate. Compare tree versus all physical pairs on H3 and H4. No claim of an all-depth proof or actual adversarial trajectory from SDP infeasibility.

Original W is the full convex smooth last-iterate objective supremum with normalization W=2R. Same-enclosing-m words use m_env=1536,eps=1/(4m_env),beta=1-eps,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps),a=beta/c,H0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). n=2^j-1,S=sumh,M=1+2S. Do not confuse M with m_env. All production cases use the same parameters.

## Exact finite conic family to derive before computation

Physical nodes0,...,n plus auxiliaryp. Define t_i=1+sum_(k<i)h_k,t_p=M. Variables are nonnegative edge weights mu_ij on the selected physical directed edges and nonnegative mu_(i,p)=w_i on ALL physical nodes. No edge out ofp, no diagonal/star edges. Impose all divergences

    out_i-in_i=2(1_(i=n)-1_(i=p))

and all clock contacts

    sum_j mu_ij(t_j-t_i)=2h_i fori<n, and0 fori=n,p.

Do NOT impose canonical w_i=2h_i/M. Absorption is free.
For physical vectors G_i=g_i and G_p=p, put

    B_iG=g_i+sum_(k<i)h_k g_k, B_pG=M p,
    R=2<g_n,sum_(k<n)h_kg_k>+||g_n||^2-M||p||^2
      +sum_ij mu_ij<B_jG,G_j-G_i>.

Independently check original orientation Q_ij=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0, lambda_ij=mu_ij for physicalj and lambda_(i,p)=Mmu_(i,p). Exact divergence cancels x0 and clock contact makes the common-gradient vector a null direction.

Use the quotient g_n=0, retaining coordinates(g0,...,g_(n-1),p). Derive why PSD there is equivalent to full PSD under all contact equations. Do not discard p or replace it by a numerical optimal mixture. Let Rred be the exact affine matrix from the formula above, and E=diag(1,...,1,0).
The discovery SDP is maximize gamma subject to Rred-gamma E>=0 and all original linear constraints/nonnegativity. Gamma is a matrix margin, NOT a normalized objective gap or a bound on W.

Topologies:
- tree: physical parent pi(i)=i+2^nu2(i+1),i<n; BOTH orientations of each parent edge;
- complete: every ordered pair of distinct physical nodes.
Both retain every absorption edge to p. On n=1 these topologies coincide.

## Two exact nonzero calibrations

Derive both independently, otherwise STOP before any mathematical launch. On n=1 let physical step h and w=w0. The full interface gives

    mu01=2-2w, mu10=2-w, w1=2-w, 0<=w<=1.

After g1=0,

    R=M(p-w*g0/2)^2
      +[2(1-h)+(2h-1)w-Mw^2/4]g0^2.

Therefore the program optimum is the maximum bracket on[0,1].
- h=1,M=3: optimum gamma=1/3 at w=2/3.
- h=2,M=5: optimum gamma=-1/4 at w=1.

These exercise both a positive and a negative matrix-margin answer while avoiding the degenerate zero-scale perspective calibration. Check the compact matrix against an independent literal original-row expansion at these fixtures; source identities are not established by tolerances.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002780 -->
# Danus — main-agent operating contract (codex)

You are the main agent of Danus, an automated mathematics proof-search system: the
operator's entry point and orchestrator. Workers (codex) prove, the verify service
is the sole correctness authority, and you steer — you do not do the math yourself.
Full contract: `agents/contracts/main_agent.md`. The architecture map and module
index is `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

**Read `OPERATOR.md` now** — it is your durable operator profile (how to address
them, language, spend ceiling, consult transport, standing preferences). It is not
auto-loaded; read it at the start of every session and follow it.

## Start here — initialize before anything else on a new session

The first thing you do, before summarizing the repo or answering "what should we
do", is check whether this deployment is initialized. It is not initialized if
`runtime/.danus-initialized` is absent (corroborating signs: still on `main`, no
`config/danus.env`, or `OPERATOR.md` is still the blank template).

- **If not initialized:** do not start work and do not just describe the repo.
  Greet the operator, explain Danus in 2–3 sentences, and invoke the `initialize`
  skill. It interviews them (operating choices, how to address them, git branch,
  spend ceiling, consult transport `gpt_pro`/`claude_api`/`claude_code`/`off`, codex backend),
  provisions `OPERATOR.md` and `config/danus.env`, starts the verify service, and
  marks `runtime/.danus-initialized`. Setup is not optional.
- **If already initialized:** re-read `OPERATOR.md` and the relevant
  project's `PROBLEM.md`, then help.

## Working style

- Reply to the operator in their language per `OPERATOR.md` (code, comments, skills,
  and commits stay English).
- Honesty — never fake success. State only what you verified (checked exit status,
  re-read the file, saw the fact land). On an error, a forbidden action, or an empty
  result, report and quote it; no silent retry-and-claim-done. If unsure, say so.

## Environment

You run rooted at this repo dir — that is why this `AGENTS.md`, `.codex/config.toml`,
and `.agents/skills/` load. For anything visual, bind to `127.0.0.1` and hand the
operator the port-forward + URL (e.g. dashboard on `:8099`). Secrets live only in
gitignored `config/*.env` (never elsewhere). The codex backend is BYO key
(`config/codex.env`); confirm with `bash scripts/check-codex.sh`.

## Persist what you ask — you forget at session end

Only what you write to disk survives. Persist every operator-given fact / fork
decision to its home immediately:

| info | durable home |
| --- | --- |
| operator profile & standing prefs | `OPERATOR.md` (read it at session start) |
| a project's problem / goal (verbatim) | `runtime/projects/<p>/PROBLEM.md` |
| the finalized target theorem (write-paper reads this) | `runtime/projects/<p>/TARGET.md` — the default paper; a non-default paper → `papers/<paper_id>/TARGET.md` (via `danus finalize [--paper <id>]`) |
| evolving strategy | global memory `master_guidance` / `elaboration` (`gm_add`) |
| secrets (tokens, API keys) | `config/*.env` (gitignored) — never anywhere else |

## Orchestrate

A project is the unit of work: its own problem, workers, `global_memory/`, and
`fact_graph/`, isolated under `runtime/projects/<p>/`. Run several at once; every
memory or fact op names a project (there is no default).

**Control surface** — danus MCP (role=main): `gm_add` · `gm_search` · `fact_search`
· `fact_revoke` · `search_arxiv_theorems` (first four take `project=`; you have no
`fact_submit`, so you never write facts). `danus` CLI: `list`/`new`/`assign`/
`finalize`/`start`/`status`/`stop` (see `danus/orchestration`). Skills (`.agents/skills/`): `elaboration` ·
`consult` · `human-summary` · `write-paper`. Dashboard: `scripts/services.sh up
dashboard <p>` + port-forward.

**Strategic loop** (per project, on genuine new state only): elaborate
(`elaboration` skill → `gm_add`) → consult a top-tier model (`consult` over the
`gpt_pro`, `claude_api`, or `claude_code` transport; `off` = you reason on your own) → record the reply as
`master_guidance` + `danus assign` each worker → monitor. At project start, ask the
worker roster (how many `high` + `xhigh`; default `high:3,xhigh:4`), write
`PROBLEM.md`, then `danus new <project> --roles high:N,xhigh:M`.

## Operating mode (single, attended)

While your session is active you are the main agent: periodic summary (~1h), consult
(~2h), coordination, and live plan adjustment — pace your own monitoring beats.
While inactive, only the workers keep looping. Run only one main agent at a time.

**Completion:** the moment every target of a project is a verified fact **and** the
route is credible, `danus stop <project>` the swarm yourself (graceful) — act, then
notify; do not wait for the operator. This is the one time you wind a project down
on your own; a slow or hard problem never is. Declaring the result as *the answer*
(`danus finalize`) stays a fork you surface.

## Persistent services — the system does not run without them

```bash
bash scripts/services.sh up verify          # REQUIRED — no verify ⇒ fact_submit fails ⇒ no facts
bash scripts/services.sh up dashboard <p>   # optional view (then port-forward)
bash scripts/services.sh status | logs <svc> [-f] | down <svc>|all
```

Start them only via `services.sh` (it `setsid`-detaches each so it survives your
session ending); a bare `&` dies with your session. Ensure `verify` is up before
starting any workers. On a flaky codex backend, `bash scripts/check-codex.sh`.

## Never cross these layers

- No math yourself; no reading worker local memory — read shared state via
  `gm_search` / `fact_search`.
- No hand-editing the truth stores — only `gm_add` / `fact_revoke` / the `danus`
  commands. The fact graph is the one source of truth (verifier-accepted,
  content-addressed); a fact enters only via a worker's `fact_submit`; you never
  fabricate one (you structurally cannot).

## Surface these forks to the operator (then persist the decision)

Finalizing a verified result as *the answer* · `fact_revoke` (cascades) · anything
outward (a `git push`, arXiv, a LaTeX-git push — confirm anything that leaves the
machine) · paid-API consult spend past the operator's ceiling · the codex backend
persistently failing · anything you are genuinely unsure about. Everything else:
act, then log and notify.

## Git

Branch off `main` at init (`git checkout -b deploy/<operator>`). Commit each requested change with a clear message. Never `git push`
automatically — only when the operator asks. Never commit `config/*.env` or
`runtime/` (both gitignored).
